# #705 — entry at the breakout close × four stops × up to two retries, under his carried exit: what keeps the good setups in for the run, and what it costs on every breakout against ordinary stocks (2017–21 and 2022–24; read 2026-10-22)

**MEASUREMENT ONLY. $0, local data, no network. Nothing here changes or proposes to change a strategy, an entry or exit rule,
a stop, a size, a safeguard or any live trade state — those are the operator's (THE LINE). Every cell of the grid below is
reported; none is picked.**

**The sealed 2025–26 table is untouched.** No 2025–26 file was opened; the 2022–24 export is cut at 2024-12-31 at load
(asserted); every trade whose walk runs past the last readable session is marked at that close and flagged, never read further.

His goal, verbatim (2026-10-22): *"I wouldn't expect to catch 100%, that's not the goal, but the really good setup ones we
shouldn't miss or stopped before the run, even if we need to retry."* Getting stopped out is *"a stop placement problem or a
reentry problem, or entry timing but not that there's no edge in breakouts"*; *"False breakout happen, that's why we take tight
stop losses"*; the edge is *"which breakout to pick (the A+ setups), but also positive risk v reward, taking small losses and
making huge returns when right"*; the comparison must measure *"buy a stock at the precise moment it gives the best risk to
reward"*, not drift over weeks. A breakout = the stock leaves the consolidation and enters expansion; the breakout day =
*"range expansion (up)"*; ~55 breakouts a week is *"ridiculous"*, cut ~90%.

## 0. What this grid can and cannot say — read before any number

- **Daily bars cannot order a stop and a target inside one session.** A bar that touches both books the STOP (the carried
  exit's own pessimistic rule). Every R here is therefore a floor on the tight stops and closer to exact on the wide ones,
  where a one-bar touch of both is rare. Intraday entries (ORH5) are not in this grid; the entry is the breakout CLOSE.
- **The doublers (P-win) were chosen after the fact.** They say what a stop × retry rule HOLDS once the stock is known to
  have doubled — never whether the rule pays. Whether it pays is the P-all vs P-ctl comparison, on every real breakout against
  ordinary stocks of the same day, volatility and prior strength given the SAME entry, stop, retry and exit rules.
- **The exit is his carried `pp`, fixed; only the entry day, the stop and the retry vary.** A wider stop changes the R unit
  and the position size at once — the account view (fixed 0.5% risk per trade) is beside every cell so the smaller size is
  visible.

## 0b. The answer, in plain words

- **(1) Which stop × retry cells keep the good setups in for the run (P-win: the 481 / 258 doublers that began with a measurable
  breakout, entered at the breakout CLOSE, under his carried exit)?** The 1-ADR stop with two retries keeps the most: **44% / 48% of the
  doublers were still in a trade when the stock had made half its gain** (213 of 481; 125 of 258). ⚠ CORRECTED after verification:
  the grid enters EVERY doubler on a breakout day picked with hindsight, so 44% is not comparable with what we actually did across all
  481. The fair comparisons are: (a) of the doublers we actually entered, 11% / 13% were still in at half the move (25 of 237; 14 of
  112); and (b) within this grid, the same entry day with no retry holds 24% / 30%. A real-time breakout row (R1) existed before half the
  move on 312 of 481 / 145 of 258 doublers; the rest of the gap is detection and entry coverage, not stop or retry. Every cell's detail is in §1; the shape:

  | cell (entry at the breakout close) | still in at half the move, 2017–21 | 2022–24 | kept ≥ ¼ of the gain | kept ≥ ½ | retries used (mean) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | S1 breakout-day low, no retry (n = 481 / 258) | 118 (25%) | 76 (29%) | 65 (14%) / 43 (17%) | 4 (1%) / 6 (2%) | — |
  | S1, up to 2 retries | 207 (43%) | 112 (43%) | 111 (23%) / 64 (25%) | 10 (2%) / 9 (3%) | 0.74 / 0.54 |
  | S2 entry − 1 ADR, no retry | 117 (24%) | 78 (30%) | 69 (14%) / 43 (17%) | 4 (1%) / 5 (2%) | — |
  | **S2, up to 2 retries** | **213 (44%)** | **125 (48%)** | **121 (25%) / 70 (27%)** | 8 (2%) / 9 (3%) | 0.66 / 0.61 |
  | S3 entry − 1.5 ADR, up to 2 retries | 136 (28%) | 96 (37%) | 85 (18%) / 55 (21%) | 5 (1%) / 5 (2%) | 0.28 / 0.29 |
  | S4 base low, any retries | 45 (9%) | 26 (10%) | 28 (6%) / 11 (4%) | 7 (1%) / 5 (2%) | 0.01 / 0.00 |

  - **Retries are what lift it.** Without a retry, every stop keeps a quarter to a third in; the first retry adds 9–15 points and the
    second another 4–8 at S1 / S2 (at S3: +6 / +8, then +1 / +1). Of the bases in at the half-way point under S2 with retries, 96 of 213
    / 47 of 125 got there on a retry (those whose first trade was not the one open then).
  - **The wide stops lose to the EXIT, not the stop.** Under the carried exit the stall cut sells a trade that has not made +1R by the
    close of session 3. With the base low as the stop (4 ADR wide) +1R is a 4-ADR move in three days, so **94% / 93% of the doublers are
    cut at the session-3 close**; the 1.5-ADR stop is cut on 53% / 44%. The exit's R-relative legs turn a wide stop into a 3-day time stop.
  - **No cell keeps half the gain on more than 3% of doublers.** The ⅓ sold at +2R and the 20-day trail end these trades long before the
    peak (median kept share of the gain 5–7% in the best cell). That is the exit's doing; the exit was held fixed here by design.
- **(2) What it costs on every real breakout, and does it beat ordinary stocks given the same rules (P-all vs P-ctl)?** On every first
  close above a base high (R1: 9,239 / 4,457 complete bases, **about 38–39 trades a week without retries, 39–71 with two**), three
  readings per era, side by side as computed (every cell in §1b):
  - **Per-base R and ADR P&L (does the chain make money against the control?).** 2017–21: roughly break-even — mean R per trade +0.02 to
    +0.08 across the cells, the median trade negative in every cell except 2017–21 S4 (+0.004, about zero); the one cell whose per-base R clears the control is **S2 with
    retries (+0.052 with one, +0.000 .. +0.110; +0.071 with two, +0.016 .. +0.136)** — ⚠ CORRECTED after verification: NOT ROBUST. The
    control's retry level (its 20-session prior high, a median ~2 ADR above its entry) is a different event from the breakouts' (the base
    high, ~0.33 ADR below entry) and fires about half as often. Re-anchored near its own entry, the per-base edge no longer clears zero.
    Only the NO-RETRY comparisons are clean, and there S2 reads +0.005 — S2 without retries +0.005 (−0.035 .. +0.050), so it
    is the retries on the stocks that came back through the base high, not the first entry; S1 with two retries clears zero in ADR units
    only (+0.068, +0.021 .. +0.119). It is not read as an edge: retries re-anchor S1's stop to an ordinary day's low, so its chains carry
    the same near-zero-stop distortion as the control (verifier, §V), and the 0.25-ADR width floor bounds S1 at no-retry only. 2022–24: **every cell loses, and so does the
    control** — mean R per trade −0.03 to −0.14 (S1 −0.11, S2 −0.11, S3 −0.08, S4 −0.03), the control −0.01 to −0.25; every per-base
    interval spans zero or sits below it; the retries make it worse per base (S2 −0.113 → −0.174).
  - **The realized tail (bases with some trade ≥ 5R, per 100).** Small in absolute terms (0 to 5 per 100 bases), and **S2 clears the
    control on the positive side in BOTH eras**: 2017–21 1.9 vs 1.4 without retries (+0.5, +0.1 .. +0.9) rising to 3.5 vs 2.1 with two
    (+1.4, +1.0 .. +1.8); 2022–24 1.6 vs 1.2 (+0.4, +0.1 .. +0.9) rising to 2.7 vs 1.7 (+1.0, +0.4 .. +1.6). S3 with retries clears zero in
    2017–21 only (+0.3, +0.2 .. +0.5); S1 and S4 read BELOW their (narrower-stopped) controls in both eras. ≥ 10R bases: S2 +0.3 to +0.5
    per 100 in 2017–21 (clear of zero), 0.0 to +0.1 in 2022–24 (spanning zero).
  - **The race (does the first trade reach +5R before its stop more often? — risk / reward at the moment).** No, in either era, where the
    stop is the same object on both sides: S2 **17.8% vs 18.7%** (2017–21) and **13.6% vs 13.7%** (2022–24); S3 14.8 vs 14.9 and 10.4 vs
    10.4. The realized-tail lift at S2 therefore does not come from reaching +5R before the stop more often; it sits in what the chain
    holds after that point (the trail) and in the retries. The number is stated, not interpreted further.
  - **The expansion-day subset R2 (6.5 / 7.4 trades a week, the ~90% cut) is not different from R1 against its own control** in either
    era (every per-base interval spans zero; 2017–21 S2 with two retries +0.108, −0.045 .. +0.279; 2022–24 −0.036, −0.163 .. +0.105).
  - **Account view (0.5% of equity risked per trade, additive, no position cap):** at 38–71 trades a week the book holds **19–66 positions
    at once (median), up to 820**; 2017–21 totals +73% to +660% with drawdowns of 32% to 493% of starting equity; 2022–24 −67% to
    −542% with drawdowns 74% to 792%. The wider the stop the smaller the swing (S4: +73% / 32% drawdown; −67% / 74%). The matched control
    book reads the same order of magnitude. These are the sums of 0.5% × R, not a compounding account — they say the swings, not a return.
- **So (corrected):** within the grid, a re-entry roughly doubles how many good-setup winners are held to half their move (a quarter → nearly half),
  and the day-3 stall cut is what undoes a wide stop. On every real breakout, given the same entry, stop, retry and exit, the breakout
  stocks do not reach +5R before their stop more often than ordinary stocks of the same day, volatility and prior strength, in either
  era; the 1-ADR stop with retries holds a ≥ 5R trade on about one more base per 100 than the control in both eras — but the retry rows'
  control is not comparable (above), so that lift is not shown to be the breakout's; in 2022–24 that book still loses money as a whole (−0.10 to −0.17 R per base), as does the control's.
- **Action: none from this data (THE LINE).** The stop, the retry and the exit are his; no cell is picked. The registered read that
  decides next is the RS-leadership read on the sealed 2025–26 (selection); a stop × retry × exit read with a cell he names has no
  registration — whether to write one is his.

## D. Definitions — FIXED BEFORE ANY OUTCOME WAS COMPUTED (frozen; results sit below it)

The four questions of the standard, answered first:
1. **Decision served:** whether the "stopped before the run" loss on the good setups is a stop-placement / re-entry
   question (a stop × retry cell keeps most doublers in through half their move) and what that cell costs on every real
   breakout relative to ordinary stocks. What to do about it is his.
2. **What would change it:** if no stop × retry cell keeps the doublers in, the loss is not stop placement — it is the
   entry day or the exit. If a cell keeps them in but reads no better than the matched control on P-all (per-base R, ≥ 5R /
   ≥ 10R per 100, race shares), the stop is not where the edge lives — selection is. If a cell beats the control with
   intervals clear of zero in both eras, the stop / retry is a lever.
3. **Population:** §M. 4. **What would make this wrong:** (a) a stop proxy on the control that is not the same object as the
   base low on the entries (stated as a proxy, widths reported for both); (b) the pessimistic touch-both rule, which
   penalises the tight stops more; (c) censoring at the cut, which cuts 2022–24's last months (complete-only is the headline);
   (d) retries that overlap a later real breakout of the same ticker (counted as composition).

**Sessions and bars.** Session calendar = SPY's dates in each era's `daily_closes_all.csv.gz` (Alpaca split-adjusted daily
bars; 2017–21 export 2016-01-04..2022-06-30; 2022–24 export cut to ≤ 2024-12-31 at load, asserted). ADR20 at a session = mean
(high − low) / close over the 20 sessions ending there (a fraction). SMA20 = the mean close over the 20 sessions ending at the
session (the stock's own closes, before and after the entry). Float32 bar frame; a strict `>` at the base high is compared at
float32 (the prior build's tie trap).

**POPULATIONS (one row per base; a chain of up to three trades per base).**
- **P-win** — the +100% doubler episodes of `cb_big_winner_recall_2026-10-22.md` §D with `class = measurable_breakout`,
  not excluded, base nights covered (`wrecall_episodes_<era>_x200` × `wrecall_catch_<era>_x200.covered = 1`): **481 / 258**.
  Breakout day b = `b_p` (the first close above the base's highest high before a quarter of the move), base high =
  `base_high_p`, base low = `base_low_p`, ADR20 at b − 1 = `adr20_bprev`. Episode gain = close(p) − close(t) per share; s50
  = the first session whose close had made half the gain. Trough-start by construction (b > t).
- **P-all** — every real breakout R1 of `cb_breakout_definition_2026-10-22.md` §D (`bdef_r1_<era>`: the first close above
  the base high `pivot_full`, fixed at the watch night D, within sessions 0..59 of a published C0 `tl` fill; one row per
  (ticker, day t)): **9,281 / 5,530** before the completeness cut. **R2** = its expansion-day subset (`r2_exp = 1`: range ≥ 1
  ADR20, close in the top 30%, volume ≥ 1.5× the 50-day): **1,582 / 1,073**. Base low = the watch row's `base_low`; ADR20
  at t − 1 = `adr_t1`.
- **P-ctl** — the matched control of the same doc (`bdef_control_<era>` rows on the R1 entry days: every export ticker
  with, at t − 1, adjusted close ≥ $5, 20-session median dollar volume ≥ $10M, 130 consecutive sessions, ticker
  `^[A-Z]{1,5}$`; tickers in any entry set on t or with a watch row at t − 1 excluded), re-walked here under the SAME entry
  price rule, stop rule, retry rule and exit. Matching: each entry's control value = the control's mean in its own (t,
  ADR20 decile, 63-session-return quintile at t − 1) cell, deciles and quintiles cut on THAT entry set (R1, then R2); an
  empty cell falls back to (t, decile), then to t; the fallback counts are reported. **A control stock has no base, so two
  proxies are stated here, not chosen later:** its "base high" = the highest high over the 20 sessions [t − 20, t − 1]; its
  "base low" = the lowest low over the same 20 sessions (the R1 bases' median length is 12 sessions, the doublers' 25–27;
  20 is a middle). The same 20-session proxy is computed on the ENTRIES beside their real base low so the width gap is
  visible.
- **Account-view control** = one control row per entry drawn at random (seed 702) from the entry's own cell, walked
  identically — a synthetic book of the same size and the same entry days.

**ENTRY.** `close`: at the close of the breakout day t (session 0; nothing can happen on session 0 — the entry is its last
print). `nopen` (beside): at the open of t + 1, which is then session 0 and IS walked (its low can stop, its high can reach
the target or +1R; the open cannot be a gap partial since it is the fill). A `nopen` entry at or under its stop (R ≤ 0) is
skipped and counted; so is a `close` entry whose close equals the day's low under S1.

**STOPS (R = entry − stop; the width in ADR = R / (ADR20 at t − 1 × entry), median reported per population).**
S1 = the breakout day's low (the low of day t in BOTH entry variants). S2 = entry − 1.0 × ADR20(t − 1) × entry. S3 = entry −
1.5 × ADR20(t − 1) × entry. S4 = the base low (uncapped; the control's 20-session proxy). The stop is never capped.

**RETRIES.** T0 none. T1 / T2 = up to one / two re-entries. A re-entry is allowed only after a trade whose exit was a STOP
(the original stop OR the breakeven stop after a third — "stopped out" in his words; the share of retries that followed a
breakeven stop is reported beside; a stall cut, a trail exit or a horizon mark never earns a retry). The re-entry = the first
session in [x + 1, x + 20] (x = the stop-out session) whose CLOSE > the base high (float32 strict), entered at that close
(`nopen`: at the next open), with the SAME stop rule re-anchored to that day (S1 = that day's low, S2 / S3 on ADR20 of the
day before, S4 = the base low unchanged). T1 is T2's chain cut after the second trade; T0 is its first trade (one walk, three
readings). Trades of one base are its CHAIN; per-base R = the sum of the chain's R (equal risk per trade).

**EXIT — the carried `pp`, on daily bars, exactly the runner's `exitpb_walk` order per session j = 1..120 (E = 0):**
(0) a session that OPENS at or above fill + 2R, no third yet sold → the third sells at that open (the open precedes the low);
(1) the stop in force BEFORE the session: open ≤ stop → everything left at the open (a gap is worse than −1R), else low ≤ stop
→ at the stop; a bar touching both the stop and the target books the STOP (pessimistic) unless it opened above the target;
(1b) the +2R leg: high ≥ fill + 2R, no third yet → 1/3 sells AT fill + 2R; after any third the stop = max(stop, fill), resting
from the NEXT session; (1c) the stall cut: if no high in sessions 1..3 (`nopen`: 0..3) reached fill + 1R and the trade was not
stopped, everything sells at the session-3 close (`time_exit`); (2) the session leg: at the session-3 close, no third yet, close
> fill → 1/3 sells at that close (his ruling: only in profit; else skipped, nothing moves); (3) the trail, live once a third has
sold OR j ≥ 3: close < SMA20 → everything left sells at the NEXT open (if that open exists; else the mark below); (4) j = 120 →
marked at the close (`censored`). Bars running out before that → marked at the last readable close (`degraded`), flagged. R
per trade = Σ legs (exit price − fill) × fraction / (fill − stop0), against the ORIGINAL stop.

**COMPLETENESS (the cut).** A base is `complete` when t + 120 ≤ the last readable session (its first trade can run its full
horizon); the headline cells use complete bases only (unbiased by what exited before the cut). All bases, with marks, beside.
A retry chain that runs past the last session is marked and flagged (`chain_truncated`), counted per cell. For P-win the
headline measures need bars only to s50 and to the chain's exits; truncated chains are counted.

**MEASURES per cell (stop × retry × entry variant) and era (2017–21: watch nights 2017-03-01..2021-12-31; 2022–24:
2022-03-28..2024-12-31), composition first, n on every row, tail first.**
- **P-win:** the share of bases with some trade of the chain still open at s50 (entry ≤ s50 ≤ exit session); the share whose
  chain kept ≥ half / ≥ a quarter of the episode's per-share gain (Σ chain P&L per share / (close(p) − close(t)); equal
  shares per attempt); retries used (mean, and the share of bases that needed 1 / 2); the share stopped before s50 on the
  first trade; the chain's R (mean, median, ≥ 5R / ≥ 10R).
- **P-all (R1, R2) and P-ctl:** trades per week (chain trades ÷ (sessions of the entry span ÷ 5)); mean R per trade and per
  base; win rate (R > 0, per trade); ≥ 5R and ≥ 10R per 100 trades and per 100 bases; the race shares on the FIRST trade
  (the `01_race` convention: sessions 1..60 from the entry, first low ≤ stop vs first high ≥ entry + kR for k = 2 / 5 / 10, a
  tie → the stop, neither → marked at the 60th close in R; needs 60 sessions); P-all minus P-ctl (cell means) on per-base R,
  first-trade R, win rate, ≥ 5R / ≥ 10R per 100 bases and the race hit shares, with month-block bootstrap intervals (calendar
  month of t, 2,000 resamples, seed 702, each resample's mean = Σ drawn blocks ÷ their count).
- **Account view:** fixed 0.5% of equity risked per trade, additive (not compounded), no position cap, sequenced by exit
  session: total return = 0.005 × Σ R; worst drawdown = the deepest peak-to-trough of the running sum × 0.005; the number of
  positions open at once (median / max) as composition; the same on the account-view control.
- **Stop widths:** median (quartiles) in ADR per stop per population.
- **Parity, before any grid number is read as final:** the walker re-walks the published `pp` rows (`trades_*_exitpb`, `pp`
  / `tl` / `orh5` / C0 / mature, no day-0 stop and no day-0 third, exit inside the readable bars) from their published fill
  and stop on daily bars from session 1, day-0 reach read from E's daily high (a proxy for the minute high after the fill),
  and must reproduce `realized_r` and `exit_session`; the residual mismatches are counted and explained or the build stops.

**Why race horizon 60 and exit horizon 120 differ:** the race is the `01_race` convention (a fixed target-or-stop over 60
sessions, comparable to that read); the `pp` exit has the runner's 120-session horizon.

Reading it: under S1 / S2 the first trade is stopped before the half-way point on 44–45% / 36–43% of doublers and cut at session 3 on
another 21–31%; the retries recover a third to a half of the stopped ones. On these bases a stopped first trade almost always gets its retry (97–100%
of stop-outs see a close back above the base high within 20 sessions — they are doublers, the stock comes back by construction); what
the table measures is whether the retry then HOLDS. Under S3 the stall cut, not the stop, is the main exit (53% / 44%), and under S4 it
is nearly the only one (94% / 93%): +1R at a 4-ADR stop is a 4-ADR move in three sessions. The retries hardly fire under S4 because a
base-low stop-out is a failed base (6 / 1 retries in all). The next-open entry reads 2–6 points lower on "still in" in every cell
(generated tables).

### 1b. P-all vs P-ctl — what it costs on every real breakout, against ordinary stocks given the same rules (R1 = every first close above a base high; COMPLETE bases, t + 120 ≤ the last readable session; control = the mean of the entry's (day, ADR decile, 63-session-return quintile) cell; intervals = month-block bootstrap of the per-entry difference, seed 702, 2,000)

Columns, tail first: trades per week counts every chain trade over the complete entries' span (244 / 114 weeks); "≥ 5R bases per 100" =
bases with some trade ≥ 5R, entries vs control; "≥ 5R / ≥ 10R" per 100 TRADES on the entries; then "per base" (a base's chain summed) and
"mean R per trade" (the mean over every trade of the cell, entries vs the matched control); "race +5R" = the first trade's high reached entry + 5R before its
low reached the stop (sessions 1–60, a tie → the stop); "first-trade exit" = stopped / cut at session 3 / trailed; "stops under 0.25 ADR"
= the share of first trades whose stop is that narrow (the R unit inflates on them, §1c); the account view = 0.5% × R summed in exit
order: total / worst drawdown / positions open at once median (max).

Reading it, tail first. The ≥ 5R bases per 100 and the +5R race are the risk / reward at the moment: at S2 and S3, where the stop is
the same object on both sides, the breakouts and the control reach +5R before their stop at the same rate in both eras (2017–21: 17.8%
vs 18.7%, 14.8% vs 14.9%; 2022–24: 13.6% vs 13.7%, 10.4% vs 10.4%). At S1 and S4 the control's stop is a different object (an ordinary
day's low; a 20-session low) and reads narrower, so its R-unit numbers are not comparable (§1c). The per-base R intervals that clear
zero: 2017–21 S2 with one retry (+0.052, +0.000 .. +0.110) and two (+0.071, +0.016 .. +0.136), and S1 with two in ADR units (+0.068,
+0.021 .. +0.119); 2022–24 none on the positive side. The median trade is negative in every cell of both eras (2017–21 S1 −0.28, S2
−0.16, S3 −0.02, S4 0.00; 2022–24 −0.46, −0.45, −0.10, −0.02): the mean rides on a few trades (2017–21 S1 without retries, the five
best trades are 59% of the total R; with two retries 84%).

### 1c. Composition and the two mechanics the grid exposes

- **Stop widths (first trade, median and quartiles, in ADR20 at t − 1 × entry).** 2017–21 — doublers: S1 1.13, S4 4.55; R1: S1 0.89
  (0.61 .. 1.25), S4 3.96 (3.07 .. 5.22); control: S1 0.43 (0.22 .. 0.71) with **25% of its stops under 0.25 ADR** (an ordinary day's low
  sits near its close far more often than a breakout day's does), S4 2.88 (1.65 .. 4.40) with 80,395 of 2.1M rows skipped because the
  20-session low sat above the close. 2022–24 the same shape (generated composition table). S2 / S3 are 1.0 / 1.5 ADR on both sides by
  construction — the only two stops where entries and control are the same object.
- **Why the S1 control's R is not comparable.** A 2-cent stop under a $40 close makes R = 2 cents; the next 1-ADR move is then 50R or
  −50R. The control's mean R per trade swings from +0.243 (2017–21) to −0.246 (2022–24) at S1 on that tail alone; its ADR-unit P&L
  (+0.030 / −0.076 per base) does not. The sensitivity above (both sides ≥ 0.25 ADR) brings S1's control to +0.046 / −0.151 per trade.
- **The stall cut is R-relative, so it scales with the stop.** First-trade exits (stopped / cut / trailed): S1 65 / 25 / 10%, S2 62–65 /
  24–26 / 11–12%, S3 34–37 / 54–56 / 9–10%, S4 3 / 95 / 2% (both eras within a point). The wide stops' lower drawdown is mostly the cut:
  almost every S4 trade is three sessions long.
- **Retries.** On the R1 breakouts, 75–83% of S1 / S2 stop-outs see a close back above the base high within 20 sessions (S3 68–77%,
  S4 46–62%; on the doublers 97–100%); a second retry fires on 86–134 of 481 doubler bases and on about a third of the R1 retries; 36–39% of the R1 retries followed a
  BREAKEVEN stop (after a third had sold), not the original stop; 2–3% of R1 retry entries are themselves another R1 row's breakout day
  (213 of 8,109 and 156 of 4,306 at S1), counted as both.
- **Censoring.** 2017–21: 42 of 9,281 R1 bases have t + 120 past the export's end (2022-06-30); 2022–24: 1,073 of 5,530 (entries after
  2024-07-11). The all-bases arm (open chains marked) moves no 2017–21 number by more than 0.003 R; in 2022–24 it lifts the entries' mean R
  per trade by up to +0.02 (S2 −0.113 → −0.097; the marked late-2024 chains read better than the completed ones — the same direction the
  R-decomposition doc found for its excluded fills) and the S1 control's by +0.09 (its near-zero-stop tail), so the S1 difference moves by
  −0.09 and every other stop's by ≤ 0.01 (generated tables).


## M. Method and population

**Population:** three row sets per era, derived as §D fixes them. **P-win** = the covered measurable-breakout doubler episodes of
`cb_big_winner_recall_2026-10-22.md` (`wrecall_episodes_<era>_x200.tsv.gz` × `wrecall_catch_<era>_x200.tsv.gz`, `class_p =
measurable_breakout`, `excluded = 0`, `covered = 1`): **n = 481** (troughs 2017-03-01..2021-12-31) and **n = 258** (2022-03-28..2024-12-31);
entry at the close of the breakout day `b_p`, the base high / low and ADR20 at b − 1 from the same row. **P-all** = the R1 rows of
`cb_breakout_definition_2026-10-22.md` (`bdef_r1_<era>.tsv.gz`: the first close above the watch night's `pivot_full` within sessions 0..59
of a published C0 `tl` fill, one row per (ticker, day)): **n = 9,281** over 1,102 entry days 2017-03-03..2022-03-29 and **n = 5,530** over
641 days 2022-04-01..2024-12-31; **complete** (t + 120 ≤ the last readable session) **9,239 / 4,457** (entry days to 2022-01-04 / 2024-07-11,
244 / 114 weeks); R2 = `r2_exp = 1`: 1,582 / 1,076 (complete 1,575 / 845). **P-ctl** = the rows of `bdef_control_<era>.tsv.gz` on the R1
entry days (the setup's universe at t − 1, entry / watch tickers excluded, as that doc states): **2,096,517 / 1,469,399** rows (complete
2,080,955 / 1,195,658), re-walked here; rows whose proxy stop sits at or above the close are skipped and counted (S1 20,105 / S4 80,395 in
2022–24; the 2017–21 counts are in the composition table). Bars: each era's `daily_closes_all.csv.gz` (Alpaca split-adjusted daily bars;
2017–21 export 2016-01-04..2022-06-30; **2022–24 cut to ≤ 2024-12-31 at load, asserted**); SPY's session calendar. Every episode and R1 row
was re-checked against the bars before any walk (the breakout date = the calendar's date at its index; the close and ADR20 agree to 1e-5 /
1e-4; every R1 close is strictly above its base high at float32).

**The walk** (`esr_lib.py`): one lockstep implementation of the runner's `exitpb_walk` order on daily bars (§D EXIT), applied identically to
the three populations; the chain of up to three trades per base (§D RETRIES); the `01_race` convention on the first trade; the matched
control means with the fallback counts; month-block bootstrap on the entry month (seed 702, 2,000); the additive account view. **Parity
before any grid number** (`01_parity.py` / `.txt`): the published `pp` rows (`trades_pre_exitpb.tsv.gz` / `trades_discovery_exitpb.tsv.gz`,
`pp` / `tl` / `orh5` / C0 / mature) with no day-0 stop and no day-0 third and an exit inside the readable bars — **7,335 / 4,905 rows** —
re-walked from their published fill and stop reproduce `realized_r` to within 1e-3 (p99 2e-5; the published fill is 6-digit text) and the
exit session on **7,334 / 4,905**; the one residual is a session-3 cut the published walk took on the minute high after the fill and the
daily high reads as reached (the stated proxy).

**Files.** Probes: `scripts/probes/_702_entry_stop_retry_1022/esr_lib.py`, `01_parity.py` (+ `.txt`), `02_build.py` (+ `02_numbers_<era>.json`,
`02_build_<era>.log`, `02_run_<era>.log`, `02_sample_R{1,2}_<era>.tsv` — the account-view control draw), `03_report.py` (+ `03_report.md` =
every table, `03_headline.json`), `04_checks.py` (+ `.txt`). Tables: `apollo_data/cb702_pre/out/esr_{win,r1,ctlsample}_2017-21.tsv.gz`,
`esr_ctl_2017-21.parquet` (one row per stop × variant × retry depth × control base; 50.3M rows) and the same under `apollo_data/cb702/out/`
for 2022–24 (`_smoke`-suffixed copies there are the one-cell smoke run on a 40,000-row control subsample, superseded). Nothing pinned was
written or moved; the runner and the once-only stages were neither run nor imported; no 2025–26 file was opened; no network; no subagent.

## L. What this does not answer

- **Whether a different EXIT keeps the gain.** The exit was held at his carried `pp` on purpose; its R-relative legs (the +1R-by-session-3
  cut, the +2R third) are what neuter the wide stops and cap the kept share at 2–3% of doublers. A stop × exit grid is a different read
  and the exit is his.
- **Intraday order.** A bar touching both the stop and the target books the stop (the carried exit's own rule); the tight stops carry the
  cost of that convention, the wide ones hardly. The published ORH5 entry (intraday) is not in this grid; the entry here is the daily close.
- **The S1 and S4 controls are proxies.** An ordinary stock has no breakout day and no base; the control's S1 is its ordinary day's low
  (narrower, 25% under 0.25 ADR) and its S4 the 20-session low (one in twenty rows skipped). The R-unit comparison at those two stops is
  not like for like; the ADR-unit column and the ≥ 0.25-ADR sensitivity bound it, and S2 / S3 are the clean comparisons.
- **Same-day conditioning.** R1 conditions on an up close; the control does not (the breakout-definition doc's caveat applies). The 63-
  session-return match and the next-open variant bound the effect; a residual same-week momentum effect cannot be excluded.
- **Censoring at the cut.** 2022–24's last six months are out of the headline (complete bases only); the all-bases arm reads better at
  S1 — the direction of the bias is known, its size is the gap shown, and nothing past 2024-12-31 was read to close it.
- **Survivorship**, as in every read on this export (the Alpaca universe at export time); the doublers and the control share it.
- **Selection.** Every entry set is derived from the published C0 `tl` fills' bases; stricter cells, his stream grades and RS leadership
  are not re-read here. The 2017–21 S2-with-retries interval that clears zero is one cell in one era (76% of its troughs are 2020) and does
  not repeat in 2022–24.
- **Account view is additive.** 0.5% × R summed in exit order with no position cap and no compounding: it shows the size of the swings at
  38–71 trades a week, not a return anyone would book.
- **Not the sealed 2025–26.** Nothing after 2024-12-31 was read; the registered RS-leadership read stays unspent.

## V. Verification (2026-10-22)

- **Two independent verifiers (recount + adversarial logic), both `holds_with_corrections`; 86 of 88 recounted quantities match to the
  digit. Corrections applied above:**
  - (material) The doubler comparison mixed detection with stop / retry. Restated: we were in at half-way on 11% / 13% of the
    doublers we entered; 312 / 145 doublers had a real-time breakout row before half the move.
  - (material) The S2-with-retries per-base edge is not robust: the control's retry level is a different event. Only the no-retry
    comparisons are clean.
  - (material) The S1 retries carry the same near-zero-stop distortion as the control. The S1 T2 sensitivity row is not read as
    clearing zero.
  - (minor) The count of bases in at half-way on a retry is 96 / 47.
  - (minor) The per-retry lift is stated for S1 / S2, and S3 given.
  - (minor) The 2017–21 S4 median trade is about zero, not negative.
  - (minor) The R2 count is 1,076.
  - (minor, stated not fixed) About 16% of R1 bases have another R1 row of the same stock within 20 sessions, so the trades-per-week
    and open-position counts double-count some stocks.
  - (minor, stated not fixed) The control stop-width quantiles include skipped rows; the valid-row S4 widths are 2.88 / 2.75 ADR.
  - (minor, stated not fixed) A missing bar inside a walk is marked degraded at the last readable close.
  - (minor, stated not fixed) §V (f)'s fallback counts are all-entries counts, not equal after the completeness filter.


- **Parity with the runner's published `pp` walk:** 7,334 of 7,335 (2017–21) and 4,905 of 4,905 (2022–24) published rows reproduced (R
  within 1e-3, exit session equal); the one residual is the day-0 minute-high proxy (`01_parity.txt`).
- **Own code, no walker import (`04_checks.py` / `.txt`):** (a) a plain per-trade Python walk of the §D exit on 400 random trades per
  table × 3 tables × 2 eras (every stop, variant and attempt) — 0 mismatches beyond the 7-digit text rounding (max |ΔR| 1e-4) once the
  inputs are the bar frame's float32 prices (a first pass fed float64 text and found knife-edge ties — a high exactly at +2R at float32 —
  which the table resolves at the frame's precision, stated); (b) the retry rule re-derived from raw closes on 38,536 / 20,875 R1 retries
  and 1,613 / 825 doubler retries — 0 violations, 0 stopped chains with an unused retry; (c) the P-win still-in-at-s50 / kept-½ / kept-¼
  counts recomputed from the trade table for two cells per era — equal; (d) the race recounted on 300 random first trades × 3 thresholds
  per era — 0 mismatches; (e) 200 of 200 next-open fills are the next session's open, 200 of 200 close fills the breakout close; (f) the
  matched control mean per base for S2 close T0 recomputed by a merge — equal to the digit (+0.0464 / −0.0925), n and fallbacks equal.
- **Two mechanics found on the smoke run, before any number was read as final, and carried into the design rather than patched over:**
  the control's near-zero S1 widths (an ADR-unit column and a width-floor sensitivity were added to §D's MEASURES and to every table), and
  the stall cut's scaling with the stop (the exit-kind mix was added to every cell). Neither changes a rule; both are reported.
- **Not independently verified by a second agent** (this read ran without subagents by instruction); the generated tables and the checks
  file are the material for one.
